  - **No further precondition/gating logic added here** - this bullet is pure wiring; whether anything actually starts (Claude/OAuth) or does nothing (any other configuration - see `initialise_claude()`'s own three-outcome branch, above) is entirely `claude_interface.py`'s own decision, unchanged by this.
  - **This file still has no entry at all for `claude_session_service.py` itself**, or for the broader `interfaces/`/`services/` restructuring, or for `utils_agents/agent_tools.py`'s current file (its rewrite history is recorded under §5 Phase 4 above, but not its relocation, if any). Still flagged as a known documentation debt, not addressed by this turn either.
- **Where:** `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/utils_agents/agent_interface.py`, `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/utils_agents/interfaces/claude_interface.py`, `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/initialise.py`, `bot_sanctuary/README.md`.

### FIX — `chat_call.py` still called `query_llm()` with the retired `cwd` keyword, breaking every turn — found via a full pipeline dry-run trace, fixed 2026-09-14

**Context.** Requested as a verification pass, not a hardening pass ("we are testing the pipeline, not hardening it") - a full startup → message 1 (open session) → message 2 (resume session) → shutdown trace was walked through by reading every file in the actual call chain (`main.py` → `initialise.py` → `queue.py`/`message_handler.py` → `session_worker.py` → `call_dispatch_handler.py` → `chat_call.py` → `agent_interface.py` → `claude_interface.py` → `claude_session_service.py` → `agent_tools.py`), not run live. This surfaced one genuine, pipeline-breaking regression left over from the `cwd` → `session_dir` rename (two entries above, and the earlier "update all cwd to session_dir in agent_interface and claude_interface" turn) - `chat_call.py` was never included in that rename, despite being the one real caller that matters (the only Call actually wired to `call_dispatch_handler.py`'s dispatch loop today).

- [x] **Bug: `chat_call.py::handle()`'s only call to `query_llm()` still passed `cwd=session_dir`** - a keyword argument `query_llm()` no longer has (renamed `session_dir` when `agent_interface.py`/`claude_interface.py` were updated). Raised `TypeError` on **every single turn**, with no exception - caught by `call_dispatch_handler.py::dispatch_call()`'s own `try/except` around `call.handle(...)`, so it never crashed a `SessionWorker`'s thread, but it meant the LLM was never actually reached at all: every message received a generic `{"type": "error", "error_type": "call_pipeline_failed", ...}` reply instead of a real one. This is why the earlier "cwd → session_dir" rename turns, though each individually correct and complete for the files they touched, left the pipeline as a whole broken - `chat_call.py` was outside both turns' stated scope (`agent_interface.py`/`claude_interface.py` only), but was the one file whose own call site actually needed updating to match.
  - **Fixed:** `cwd=session_dir` → `session_dir=session_dir`. One-line change, no other logic touched.
  - **Docstring/Notes updated in the same file to match current reality, not just the rename** - the `session_dir` `Args` entry and two `Notes` bullets still described the old `cwd + continue_conversation`/resume-marker mechanism from `claude_interface.py`'s pre-`claude_session_service.py` era; updated to describe the actual current routing (`claude_session_service.py`'s persistent platform for OAuth, the resume-marker mechanism for API), and to flag that `persona` (loaded from `libraries/claude/chat.md`) is only actually used on the API/one-shot path - the OAuth/persistent path loads its own persona from `libraries/claude/chat.json` instead, ignoring whatever this function passes.
- [x] **Verified, not fixed (nothing was wrong): the two runtime-configuration prerequisites the same trace flagged as "must be true for the pipeline to work" are both already correctly satisfied.** Checked directly against `compose.dev.yml`, both branches of `bot_sanctuary/setup.sh::bs_dev_run_docker()` (exposed/`dev_path` and non-exposed), and the real (gitignored) root `config.ini`:
  - `LLM_CHAT_TYPE` - wired through in all three places (`compose.dev.yml`'s `bot-sanctuary` service, both `setup.sh` `docker run` branches), and set to `"claude"` in `config.ini` (`CHATBOT_LLM_CHAT_TYPE`).
  - `LLM_CLAUDE_ACCESS_TYPE`/`LLM_CLAUDE_TOKEN` - likewise wired through all three places, and set in `config.ini` to `"OAUTH"` plus a real (non-placeholder) token value - not echoed here; `config.ini` is confirmed listed in `.gitignore`, so this was never at risk of being committed.
  - **Conclusion: `initialise_claude()` will actually select the OAuth branch and start `claude_session_service.py` on a real deployment using this `config.ini`** - the pipeline's LLM-reaching path is not blocked by configuration, only by the bug above (now fixed).
- **Not verified here, deliberately out of scope:** whether the OAuth token's own value is still valid/unexpired, whether the `claude` CLI's underlying subprocess can actually connect from inside the container, and whether RabbitMQ/Redis are reachable at the moment this runs - all genuine runtime dependencies, none of them a code-level concern this pass was checking for.
- **Where:** `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/utils_calls/chat_call.py` (the fix); `compose.dev.yml`, `bot_sanctuary/setup.sh`, root `config.ini` (verification only, no changes needed).

### New — diagnostic tool-use logging (`claude_session_service.py`, `claude_interface.py`), added 2026-09-14 to resolve a live contradiction

**Context.** After the fix above, a live query against the bot came back claiming "it has tested and verified it does not have WebSearch access" - directly contradicting the earlier static-analysis conclusion in this file's own history that `libraries/claude/chat.json`'s `"tools": ["WebSearch"]` is correctly wired into `ClaudeAgentOptions.tools`. Investigating further surfaced a real, separate gap: neither `claude_session_service.py::_run_turn()` nor `claude_interface.py::_run_query()` ever inspected anything in an `AssistantMessage`'s `content` list besides `TextBlock` - a `ToolUseBlock` (Claude actually invoking a tool) or `ToolResultBlock` (what came back) would be silently skipped by both loops, with zero trace in the logs either way. Concretely: there was no way to tell, from this application's own logs, whether the model ever attempted `WebSearch` at all, versus it succeeding, erroring, or the model just self-reporting incorrectly - an LLM has no privileged introspection into its own tool wiring beyond what it's told and what actually happens when it tries. **User-confirmed, ruling out one candidate explanation:** the bot runs under the same Claude subscription as the assistant performing this work, so an account/plan-level web-search restriction was ruled out as the cause - narrowing the remaining candidates to a stale/pre-existing persistent connection, a genuine SDK-version behavioural gap, or the model's self-report simply being wrong.

- [x] **New logging added to both call sites, identically, for parity between the persistent (OAuth) and one-shot (API/smoke-test) paths:**
  - `claude_session_service.py::_run_turn()`'s `async for message in entry.client.receive_response():` loop, and `claude_interface.py::_run_query()`'s `async for message in claude_query(...):` loop, both gained two further `elif isinstance(block, ...)` branches alongside the existing `TextBlock` one: `ToolUseBlock` → `logger.info(f"Claude tool_use: name={block.name!r} input={block.input} ...")`, `ToolResultBlock` → `logger.info(f"Claude tool_result: tool_use_id=... is_error=... content=... ...")`.
  - Both imports added directly from `claude_agent_sdk` (`ToolUseBlock`, `ToolResultBlock`) - confirmed as real, documented dataclasses on the installed SDK (`id`/`name`/`input` and `tool_use_id`/`content`/`is_error` respectively) before adding, not guessed at.
  - **Purely diagnostic - neither block type is fed into the returned reply string**, which stays text-only in both functions, unchanged. No behavioural change to what either function returns; only what gets logged along the way.
- [x] **Module header (`claude_session_service.py`) corrected in passing - a stale claim, unrelated to this specific fix but directly contradicted by work already recorded above in this same section.** "Not yet wired into `claude_interface.py`, `utils_session/session_worker.py`, or `initialise.py`... nothing outside `utils_agents/` has been touched" was true when this module was first built (2026-09-13) but has been false since the "Claude session service lifecycle" entry earlier in this same section landed - corrected to describe the actual current wiring (`query_via_oauth()` routes here; `initialise_claude()`/`terminate_claude()` call `start_claude_session_service()`/`stop_claude_session_service()`; both wired into `initialise.py`), noting `destroy_session()` alone still has no caller.
- **Not yet done - this is instrumentation, not a diagnosis.** The next actual test run's logs need to be read to determine which of the three remaining candidate explanations (stale connection, SDK-version gap, or an incorrect self-report) is real - not resolved by this entry itself.
- **Resolved, same day - root cause found, fix not yet applied.** The live logs were read after the instrumentation above landed (`bot_sanctuary.log`, spanning every turn of a fresh app run). Across all 8 turns tested (including several after the user re-asked the same question 4 separate times), **not one `Claude tool_use:`/`Claude tool_result:` line ever appeared**, and every `ResultMessage`'s own `usage.server_tool_use` counter (`{'web_search_requests': 0, 'web_fetch_requests': 0}`) stayed at zero throughout - ruling out "attempted and errored" and "stale connection ignoring a chat.json change" (the connection was freshly created at app start, in this same log). This looked, at first, like the model simply never trying and then inventing an excuse when asked directly - until the user pushed back (correctly) that the same "permission" wording came back identically on four separate, independent attempts, which is not what a one-off hallucination looks like. A `WebSearch` pass (see Sources below) surfaced the actual mechanism: `ClaudeAgentOptions.tools` only controls a tool's **nominal availability** to the model - a completely separate permission layer (`permission_mode` / `allowed_tools` / `canUseTool`) governs whether an *invocation* is actually approved, and any tool not present in `allowed_tools` falls through to `permission_mode`, which in a non-interactive/headless context with no `canUseTool` callback registered defaults to denying it - per a confirmed upstream report (github.com/anthropics/claude-code issue #46250), a denied `WebSearch` call surfaces to the model as a plain denial message, not as a `ToolResultBlock` in the message stream, which is exactly why neither this application's own logging nor the `usage.server_tool_use` counter ever shows an attempt: **the call never reaches Anthropic's servers at all - it is denied locally, before dispatch.** Confirmed against this codebase directly: neither `claude_session_service.py`'s nor `claude_interface.py`'s `ClaudeAgentOptions(...)` construction sets `allowed_tools` or `permission_mode` anywhere - only `tools=...` is set in both. **Correction to this entry's own instrumentation-conclusion above:** the model's self-report was not a fabrication after all - it was accurately describing a real, consistent, local permission denial; the earlier framing here (and stated directly to the user) that this looked like an unfounded excuse is retracted.
  - **Proposed fix (not yet applied - pending confirmation):** add `allowed_tools=agent.tools`/`allowed_tools=tools` alongside the existing `tools=...` in both `ClaudeAgentOptions(...)` call sites, reusing the same list already sourced from `chat.json`/persona frontmatter rather than introducing a new list to keep in sync. Deliberately **not** proposing `permission_mode="bypassPermissions"` - that approves every tool unconditionally (`Bash`, `Write`, `Edit`, ...), directly contradicting `chat.json`'s own persona text ("You cannot: Access local files, Read/modify/upload/save files, Execute code, Control devices"); `allowed_tools` is the narrower, already-scoped fix.
  - **Where:** `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/utils_agents/services/claude_session_service.py` (`_get_or_create_entry()`), `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/utils_agents/interfaces/claude_interface.py` (`_run_query()`) - both currently construct `ClaudeAgentOptions` with `tools=...` only.
- **Where:** `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/utils_agents/services/claude_session_service.py`, `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/utils_agents/interfaces/claude_interface.py`.

## 6. Workspace / repository access

- [ ] No `session_id`-based folder structure — session IDs are ephemeral and unsuitable as a persistent directory key.
- [ ] **Confirmed — single-user assumption:** the repository directory is wide open to the container; no per-session or per-user filesystem isolation is required.
- [ ] **Confirmed: Chat (persona: Rukia) has no access to the repository/container filesystem or code tools at all.** It is a conversation/routing-only role. Only the specialised Calls (Architect/Coder/Review/Documentation) get filesystem/tool access. This is also what makes a non-whitelisted user's Chat-only restriction meaningful as an access boundary, not just a routing default.

---

## Open items, not yet decided

- [ ] Auto-retry interval (or lack thereof) for sessions pending authentication.
- [ ] Exact field name/shape for the access-tier tag on the inbound task payload — depends on `telegram_gateway`'s own whitelist item (see its `CODE_TODO.md`'s "BUG — SESSION_RESET_ALLOWED_CHAT_IDS..." entry) landing first. **Update (2026-09-10):** confirmed by the user that `coding_allowed` will use its own dedicated whitelist env var on `telegram_gateway`'s side, separate from `SESSION_RESET_ALLOWED_CHAT_IDS` (which was clarified to mean "bot admin, may trigger a global reset of every session," not a self-service/agent-access permission) — the same individuals are expected to hold both today, but the two are not structurally tied together. No change needed on this side beyond continuing to consume whatever field `telegram_gateway` ends up stamping.
- [ ] Whether the Session Registry (`session_id → role, cwd, permission_scope, status`) persists anywhere or is in-memory-only (same accepted-trade-off `bot_orchestrator` made for its own routing state — not yet confirmed this should carry over identically here, given it affects conversation continuity rather than just message routing).
- [ ] Task classification mechanism (rule-based vs. model-based), carried over unresolved from `bot_orchestrator/CODE_TODO.md` item 4 — still parked.
- [ ] **New (2026-09-12): plan only, not yet implemented - distinguishing "LLM credit/quota exhausted" from "provider API/OAuth down" in `utils_calls/turn_handler.py::run_turn()`'s error outcome.**
  - **Goal:** today, `run_turn()` can only ever report one generic `call_pipeline_unavailable`/`call_pipeline_failed` error_type, regardless of *why* the Chat Call's reply came back `None` - a rate-limited/out-of-credit provider, an expired OAuth token, and a genuinely unreachable API all look identical from here. Two real outcomes are worth telling apart specifically: **quota/credit exhausted** (`telegram_gateway` already has a purpose-built `error_type="token_exhausted"` for this - see its README's `error` payload - which sends a fixed "taking a nap" persona message, optionally with a remaining-seconds countdown) vs. **the provider/credential itself being down** (unreachable, or a 401/403 - no known "resume at" time, so a nap-style message would be misleading).
  - **Root cause of the gap:** every `<provider>_interface.py` (`claude_interface.py`, `codex_interface.py`, `deepseek_interface.py`/`qwen_interface.py`) already catches its own failure but **discards the specific reason**, collapsing every failure into a bare `None` return - `agent_interface.py::query_llm()` and every `<name>_call.py::handle()` (only `chat_call.py` wired today) both preserve that same lossy `str | None` contract on the way up. By the time `turn_handler.py::run_turn()` sees `reply is None`, there is nothing left to distinguish.
  - **Per-provider detection, as currently understood (unverified against a real failure for three of the four - flagged, not guessed at silently):**
    - **DeepSeek/Qwen** - already the strongest case: `_post_chat_completion()`'s existing `except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:` branch already has `error.code` in hand (HTTP `429` → rate limit/quota, `401`/`403` → invalid/expired key) and a `Retry-After` response header, if either provider ever sends one, would give a real nap duration - all of this is currently read and then thrown away (only logged, not returned).
    - **Codex** - `_run_query()`'s non-zero `process.returncode` + `stderr` text is the only available signal; no structured/documented exit code convention is confirmed, so distinguishing quota vs. outage here would mean brittle string-matching against undocumented CLI error text. Flagged as a best-effort heuristic at best, possibly not worth building at all for this provider specifically.
    - **Claude** - the Agent SDK's `query()` raises on failure, but its exact exception hierarchy for a rate-limit/quota vs. an authentication failure is **not yet confirmed** against the installed `claude-agent-sdk==0.2.152` - needs verifying (docs or a live failure) before any code branches on a specific exception type, rather than assumed.
  - **Proposed contract change (bottom-up, not yet started):** each provider interface's two endpoints return a small structured result instead of `str | None` (e.g. `{"text": str | None, "failure_type": None | "quota_exhausted" | "unavailable", "retry_after_seconds": int | None}`) → `agent_interface.py::query_llm()` passes it through → every `<name>_call.py::handle()` changes its return contract to match (a five-file change for contract uniformity, even though only Chat is wired into a pipeline today - mirrors this codebase's existing "uniform two-endpoint contract across all four providers" precedent in §2) → `turn_handler.py::run_turn()` maps `quota_exhausted` → `error_type="token_exhausted"` (`message=str(retry_after_seconds)` if known, else omitted) and `unavailable` → a new `error_type="provider_unavailable"` (fixed explanatory `message`, no countdown implied) - both are `error` payloads either way, since `telegram_gateway`'s own contract already accepts any `error_type` string, only `"token_exhausted"` gets the special nap-duration templating.
  - **Proposed sequencing:** (1) confirm each provider's real error surface first - DeepSeek/Qwen's HTTP status codes are already confirmed via their own published API docs, Codex/Claude are not; (2) land the structured-result contract starting with DeepSeek/Qwen, since no further research is needed there; (3) update `agent_interface.py`/all five `<name>_call.py` modules to carry the new shape through; (4) update `run_turn()`'s mapping last, once every layer beneath it has something real to report.
  - **Open questions, not yet decided:** whether a `provider_unavailable` outcome should ever retry once within the same turn before failing it (no retry logic exists anywhere in this pipeline today); whether a `quota_exhausted` outcome should pause/back off *further* turns for that same provider for a while, or just let the next turn try again and fail again on its own (today `"token_exhausted"`'s nap message is purely chat-facing text, with no actual cooldown enforced behind it); Codex's stderr-pattern-matching approach may not be reliable enough to build on at all, in which case Codex failures might just always fall into the generic `provider_unavailable`/`call_pipeline_failed` bucket rather than trying to distinguish quota from outage for that provider specifically.
- [ ] **New (2026-09-10): a possible fixed/scheduled session-reset time, configured on this side.** Raised by the user as the second of exactly two legitimate paths to a session ever being cleared — the other being `telegram_gateway`'s admin-triggered global reset (see its `CODE_TODO.md`'s new `BUG` entry at the top of the file). Nothing about this is designed yet: whether it's a single fixed wall-clock time (e.g. reset everything nightly) or a per-session TTL-style expiry, whether it would itself publish `session_reset` per affected `session_id` the same way `resync_orphaned_sessions()` already does for crash-recovery, and how it'd resolve `session_id → chat_id` for that publish given `bot_sanctuary` is deliberately identity-blind (it may not need to — `session_reset`'s payload only ever carried `task_id`, not `chat_id`, precisely so `bot_sanctuary` never needs chat identity to request one). **Partially answered (2026-09-11):** the "how would it resolve chat identity" question is now answered — it wouldn't need to; `telegram_gateway` resolves `session_id → chat_id` itself, per the "NEW — `session_clear_request` handling" entry below (built for the admin-triggered path, but the same `session_reset`-per-session-cleared shape would apply equally to a future scheduled trigger). The scheduling mechanism itself remains undesigned. Not started; tracked here as a placeholder until designed.

---

## NEW — `session_clear_request` handling (admin-triggered global session clear, counterpart to `telegram_gateway`'s `"${BOT_NAME} refresh yourself"` command)

Status: **Design finalised (2026-09-11), cross-service with `telegram_gateway` — see its own `CODE_TODO.md`'s BUG entry at the top of that file. Fully implemented (2026-09-12), in explicit staged parts, by instruction: `bot_started` (own subsection below), trigger acceptance + response (Part 3, step 1), the per-thread graceful-shutdown mechanism (`SessionWorker.retire()`, Part 3, step 2), and the sweep-in-progress accept/reject decision (Part 3, step 3) are all now implemented. A dedicated post-implementation review pass (2026-09-12, own subsection below) confirmed no deadlock risk in the new locking, and surfaced several small, not-yet-fixed gaps (unbackstopped reject-path publish failure, a bounded continuously-busy-session edge case, and an extension of the existing "stray message" accepted risk to `retire()`). Also 2026-09-12: the optional daily timed global session reset (`SESSION_RESET_TIME`) is now implemented here too, sharing the same accept/reject sweep machinery - see its own subsection below (after an initial, incorrect attempt on `telegram_gateway`'s side was fully reverted per user correction). Same day, immediate follow-up: both this application and `telegram_gateway` are now timezone-aware (`TZ`/`CHATBOT_TZ`) - see the "Timezone awareness" subsection below, which also covers the `telegram_gateway`-side changes for completeness even though that project has its own `CODE_TODO.md`. Same day, second immediate follow-up: `config.ini` (missed in the `CHATBOT_TZ` pass above) has been caught up, and every direct `datetime.now()`/`time.time()` call in either application's own code now goes through two new shared helpers, `application_time()`/`application_time_diff()` - see the "Centralised time retrieval" subsection below.** This entry replaces two earlier same-day drafts wholesale (payload schema, `shutdown_all_session_workers()` reuse, sequencing, ack reliance, duplicate-request handling, and crash recovery were each revised at least once - see `telegram_gateway/CODE_TODO.md`'s counterpart entry for the parallel history on that side).

**Known staleness, corrected by the implementation below, kept here for history:** the `session_reset` payload description in this entry's own Decisions section (`{"task_id": ..., "session_id": ..., "type": "session_reset"}`, published **per session** as each one finishes clearing) was written 2026-09-11 and was never reconciled against `telegram_gateway/CODE_TODO.md`'s own 2026-09-12 "Confirmed design correction", which explicitly disproves the per-session model — `bot_sanctuary` publishes exactly **one** `session_reset` per trigger, at accept-time, never one per session, and `session_id` was dropped from the design entirely (confirmed as serving no purpose on `telegram_gateway`'s side). This drift was identified while implementing `telegram_gateway`'s own Part 3 subsection and is noted there too. **The trigger-acceptance implementation below (2026-09-12) follows the corrected model, not the stale Decisions-section description** — the Decisions section's own payload bullet is left as-is below, marked superseded in place, rather than rewritten, per this file's own history-preservation convention.

### Blocking dependency on §5 (unchanged, still applies)

`SessionWorker._process_batch()` (§3) today only logs what it would do — no LLM call, no `completed`/`error` ever actually published. This means the new dedicated clearing method below (see Decisions) and an immediate abandon are **behaviourally identical today** — there is no real in-flight work for either to differ over yet. The scaffolding can still be built ahead of §5 (same staging precedent as §3's on-disk directory work), but its entire point only has an observable effect once §5 exists and actually completes turns.

### Goal

A whitelisted admin chat can type `"${BOT_NAME} refresh yourself"` in Telegram to trigger a global clear of every session this application is currently holding. `telegram_gateway` detects the command and whitelist-gates it; this application decides whether to accept or ignore the request, clears its own session state, and notifies `telegram_gateway` per session as each is actually cleared.

### Context

`telegram_gateway`'s own `SESSION_RESET_ALLOWED_CHAT_IDS` whitelist was found to conflate "does the *target* chat get to reset itself" with "is the *requester* an admin allowed to trigger a *global* reset" — see its `CODE_TODO.md`'s BUG entry. The corrected design moves the whitelist check to the point the command is first typed (`telegram_gateway`), and needs a new round-trip: `telegram_gateway` asks (`session_clear_request`), this application acts and clears its own state first, then tells `telegram_gateway` per session (`session_reset`).

Two design directions were explored and rejected during this same discussion, kept here for history:
- **This application enumerating every active `session_id` and handing `telegram_gateway` an explicit target list.** Rejected — `telegram_gateway` already owns the `chat_id ↔ session_id` mapping and resolves/enumerates targets itself (see its own `CODE_TODO.md`'s Part 0/Part 4).
- **`telegram_gateway` force-clearing every stuck chat on crash recovery via `_force_apply_session_reset()`.** Rejected in favour of a more precise mechanism (`bot_started`, see below) — `_force_apply_session_reset()` remains untouched, but only as a last-resort fallback, not the primary recovery path for this feature.

### Decisions

- **Inbound `session_clear_request` — accept or ignore, decided purely from whether a sweep is already in progress. Implemented 2026-09-12 as `session_worker.py::handle_session_clear_request()` — see the "Part 3, step 3" subsection below for full detail.**
  - **Accept** (no sweep currently running): snapshot every `session_id` currently in the registry (`session_worker.py`'s `_sessions`), mark a sweep in progress, and signal every one of them **concurrently** — not sequentially, not one-at-a-time-waiting. "Iteratively" in an earlier draft of this entry was corrected by the user to mean *every* session finishes on its own, independently, not one blocking the next.
  - **Ignore** (a sweep is already running): the *only* case this application ever sends a literal `completed`/`error` for this feature — closes out the *incoming* `session_clear_request`'s own `task_id` so it doesn't hang, and does nothing else. **In the accepted/normal path, no separate `completed`/`error` is ever sent** — see the `session_reset` payload note below for why one isn't needed. **Implemented as a literal `error`, not `completed`** — a decision made during implementation, not specified here beforehand; see the "Part 3, step 3" subsection for the reasoning.
  - No other business logic beyond this accept/ignore check — only a whitelisted admin can reach this at all (gated once, upstream, by `telegram_gateway`).
- **A new, dedicated `SessionWorker` method — not a reuse of `shutdown()`/`stop()` with a flag, per explicit instruction.** A third terminal path alongside the existing two:
  - `stop()` — immediate, abandons whatever's queued (unchanged, still used by `_handle_session_cleared()`).
  - `shutdown()` — graceful drain, no publish/clear on exit (unchanged, still used by `shutdown_all_session_workers()` at process-terminate).
  - ~~**New method (name TBD)** — same draining behaviour as `shutdown()` (finish everything queued, no abandonment), but its exit action always publishes `session_reset` and clears local state — no flag needed, since the method's own existence is the signal. Needs its own dedicated `threading.Event` (a third one, alongside `_stop_event`/`_shutdown_event`) and its own branch in `_run()`'s exit condition.~~ **Implemented 2026-09-12 as `retire()` — see the "Part 3, step 2" subsection below.** The "always publishes `session_reset`" half of this bullet is corrected there too, not just the name — per the same accept-time-not-per-session correction already noted above, `retire()` itself never publishes anything; the one `session_reset` for the whole sweep already went out via `session_clear_response()` (Part 3, step 1) before any session is ever signalled to retire.
  - **Re-verified: `shutdown_all_session_workers()` cannot be reused for this at all, in any form.** It only calls `worker.shutdown()` then joins each thread — no publish, no `clear_session_directory()`, no registry removal (correct for its own purpose; the process is exiting anyway). None of that carries over here, which is exactly why a genuinely new method is needed rather than a parameterised `shutdown()`.
- **Why graceful (not immediate) — recorded since it was asked directly, not just a preference:** `telegram_gateway`'s existing deferred-reset machinery (§0-§8, its own `CODE_TODO.md`) only unblocks once a `completed`/`error`-equivalent actually arrives for a chat's open task. If this application abandoned an in-flight turn instead of finishing it, that signal would simply never come, and the chat would sit deferred until `telegram_gateway`'s own timeout backstop eventually force-applies it — a real regression (silently lost reply, needlessly delayed reset). Finishing gracefully first means the common case never even needs `telegram_gateway`'s defer path to engage — by the time `session_reset` is published, the chat's last open task is already closing with it.
- ~~**`session_reset` payload — carries the session's last `task_id` when one exists, and this is deliberate, not incidental:** `{"task_id": <last task_id, if any, else null>, "session_id": <cleared>, "type": "session_reset"}`. Per the confirmed design, **`telegram_gateway` treats this message as the `completed`/`error` for that `task_id`** when one is present — this is *why* no separate `completed`/`error` is sent in the accepted path (see Decisions above): `session_reset` already does that job. Only when a session was genuinely idle (nothing queued, no `task_id` to attach) does it go out as `task_id: null`, and `telegram_gateway` resolves that chat via `session_id` instead (its own Part 0).~~
  - ~~**Re-verified: `session_id` is a new field on this payload, not already there.** `resync_orphaned_sessions()`'s current publish is `{"task_id": task_ids[0], "type": "session_reset"}` — no `session_id`. This is a real, if small, schema change shared by both publishers.~~
  - ~~**Low-cost follow-on worth doing at the same time:** `resync_orphaned_sessions()` already has `session_id` in scope in its own loop — a one-line addition gives that existing path the same resilience benefit, not required but cheap to fold in rather than leave the two publishers of the same type on different shapes.~~
  - Uses its own disposable `RabbitMQPublisher` instance per publish, same pattern `resync_orphaned_sessions()`/`_close_intermediate_task_ids()` already use — not the long-lived, thread-confined one a `SessionWorker` owns for its own turn traffic. **Still correct, kept as-is.**
  - **Superseded 2026-09-12 by `telegram_gateway/CODE_TODO.md`'s "Confirmed design correction"** (written the same day as this entry's original draft, but reconciled here only now, on implementation) — no `session_id` field, published **once per trigger at accept-time**, not once per session as each finishes. See the "Part 3, step 1" subsection below for what was actually implemented. `resync_orphaned_sessions()`'s own publish (`{"task_id": task_ids[0], "type": "session_reset"}`, no `session_id`) was in fact already correct all along under the corrected model — the "low-cost follow-on" bullet above proposing to add `session_id` to it would have been a step in the wrong direction, left un-implemented for exactly that reason.
- ~~**Sweep-in-progress tracking — entirely internal to this application, plain in-memory, never dependent on anything from `telegram_gateway`.** Each session, once it publishes its own `session_reset` and clears its own local state, **logs that it performed its reset** and reports in against a shared counter/set (the snapshot taken at accept-time). The sweep-in-progress flag clears once every snapshotted session has reported in exactly once.~~ **Implemented 2026-09-12 largely as described here** — see the "Part 3, step 3" subsection below. One correction: no session ever "publishes its own `session_reset`" (that phrasing is the same stale per-session-publish assumption marked superseded elsewhere in this entry) — a session only reports in by being removed from a plain `set[str]` (`_pending_retirements`), whose non-emptiness *is* the sweep-in-progress flag, rather than a separate boolean alongside a counter. No reliance on `telegram_gateway`'s `session_cleared` ack for this or anything else — `_handle_session_cleared()` still exists and is still idempotent against an already-removed session, but this feature does not wait on or need it.
- **No cross-process recovery of an interrupted sweep, deliberately — this is a correction from an earlier draft of this entry.** Once this application consumes/acks a RabbitMQ message, its content is gone if the process crashes before finishing — there is nothing concrete to "resume" on restart, and no attempt is made to. The in-memory sweep-in-progress flag/snapshot is simply lost on any restart, same as any other in-memory state. **New `bot_started` event (see below) is the entire extent of what this application does about a crash mid-sweep** — a one-way, unconditional "I'm alive" broadcast, with all reconciliation happening on `telegram_gateway`'s side.
- **New `bot_started` event, fired unconditionally at the very start of `initialise_application()`** (ahead of the existing `resync_orphaned_sessions()` call — see Open Questions for exact ordering confirmation) — `{"type": "bot_started"}`, no other fields. Not scoped to this feature specifically; fires on every startup regardless of cause (crash or a routine redeploy), since either way this application has no memory of what it was doing before.
- **The existing `mark_task_active`/`mark_task_complete`/`sweep_orphaned_sessions`/`resync_orphaned_sessions()` mechanism is unrelated and untouched by this feature.** It keeps doing its own generic job for ordinary orphaned tasks (admin-sweep-related or not) exactly as it does today — this feature neither relies on it nor changes it. `bot_started` is a separate, additive signal.

#### Part 3, step 1 — trigger acceptance + response — implemented 2026-09-12, by explicit instruction to implement in staged parts

Deliberately partial — this step wires the inbound/outbound round-trip only. **Not yet implemented, staying open for a later step:** the ignore-if-a-sweep-is-already-in-progress dedup, and the actual graceful per-session clearing sweep (the new dedicated `SessionWorker` exit method, sweep-in-progress snapshot/signal tracking, and this application actually clearing any of its own session state). Today, every `session_clear_request` this application receives is unconditionally treated as accepted — there is no sweep-in-progress state yet for anything to check against.

- [x] **New `message_handler.py::_handle_session_clear_request(data)`** — new `elif message_type == "session_clear_request":` branch in `process_message()`, alongside `gateway_alert`/`gateway_recover`/`session_cleared` (ahead of the generic `_dispatch_to_session()` fallback, since this payload carries no `session_id` for that path to route on). Validates `task_id` is non-empty first — logs and drops the message if not (per the confirmed design, this payload always carries a real, minted `task_id`; a missing one is malformed, not a legitimate orchestrator-triggered case here) — then delegates to `session_clear_response()`.
- [x] **New `session_worker.py::session_clear_response(task_id)`** — publishes `{"task_id": task_id, "type": "session_reset"}` immediately, via its own disposable `RabbitMQPublisher` (same pattern as `resync_orphaned_sessions()`/`_close_intermediate_task_ids()`/`_push_bot_started()`). Follows the **corrected** payload shape (no `session_id`, published once at accept-time) rather than the stale Decisions-section description above — see the "Known staleness" note at the top of this entry.
  - **Renamed from `accept_session_clear_request()`, same day, per explicit instruction** — the original name read as though this function itself made the accept/ignore decision, when it doesn't; that decision (not yet implemented) belongs to `_handle_session_clear_request()`/a future sweep-in-progress check. `session_clear_response()` names what the function actually does: send the response.
  - **`task_id` narrowed from `str | None` to `str`** — the caller (`_handle_session_clear_request()`) now validates non-emptiness before ever calling this, so it no longer needs to accept or handle `None` itself.
- [x] **Published before anything is actually cleared, deliberately** — matches `telegram_gateway/CODE_TODO.md`'s "Confirmed design correction": publishing at accept-time rather than after finishing keeps this application's own eventual graceful per-session clearing decoupled from `telegram_gateway`'s own reset-application timing, instead of forcing the two to run sequentially.
- **Known interim limitation, flagged not fixed:** if this publish fails, the admin's triggering `task_id` (on `telegram_gateway`'s side) is never closed by anything else today — no retry, no fallback. Unlike this project's other crash-recovery/ceiling-sweep mechanisms, nothing yet backstops this specific failure path. To be addressed alongside (or before) the next step, not here.
- **Where:** `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/utils_queue/message_handler.py`, `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/utils_session/session_worker.py`. No `README.md` change made yet for this step - deferred until the ignore path/actual sweep exist too, so the documented contract reflects the whole feature rather than half of it.

#### Part 3, step 2 — `SessionWorker.retire()`, the per-thread graceful-shutdown mechanism — implemented 2026-09-12, by explicit instruction to implement in staged parts

Deliberately scoped to the **per-worker mechanism only** — the third terminal path itself, not yet wired to any caller. **Not yet implemented, staying open for a later step:** snapshotting every `session_id` currently in the registry and signalling each one's `retire()` concurrently in response to an accepted `session_clear_request`, and the sweep-in-progress tracking (reporting-in counter/set) that would sit on top of that signalling. Calling `retire()` today would work correctly on whichever single `SessionWorker` it's called on, but nothing yet calls it.

- [x] **Open Question 1 resolved: named `retire()`.** Chosen over reusing "clear" (too easily confused with the already-existing, differently-scoped `clear_session_directory()`) — `retire()` names the outcome (this session is permanently done, removed from the registry) rather than the mechanism, matching `stop()`/`shutdown()`'s own outcome-focused naming.
- [x] **New `SessionWorker._clear_event` (`threading.Event`)** — a third event alongside `_stop_event`/`_shutdown_event`, set by the new `retire()` method.
- [x] **New `SessionWorker.retire()`** — sets `_clear_event`, nothing else. Same draining semantics as `shutdown()` (finishes whatever's queued rather than abandoning it) — the two differ only in their own exit action, applied in `_run()`.
- [x] **`_run()`'s exit condition widened** — `except queue.Empty:` now breaks on `self._shutdown_event.is_set() or self._clear_event.is_set()`, not just `shutdown`. Its post-loop exit action now branches: if `_clear_event` is set, calls `remove_session_worker(self.session_id)` + `clear_session_directory(self.session_id)` (both already module-level functions in this same file - no new import needed) before logging; otherwise logs the existing plain "stopped" line unchanged.
  - **Deliberately checked regardless of which condition actually broke the loop** (a full drain, or `stop()` abandoning what was left) — once `retire()` has been called at all, the intent is to permanently remove this session either way; a `stop()` arriving on top of an already-requested `retire()` doesn't cancel that intent. A narrow, accepted edge case, not specifically exercised yet: `stop()` and `retire()` both being called on the same worker is not a scenario this staged implementation constructs or tests for today.
- [x] **Corrected from the stale Decisions-section description above: `retire()` never publishes `session_reset` itself.** That publish already happened once, at accept-time, for the whole sweep (`session_clear_response()`, Part 3 step 1) - before any individual session is ever signalled to retire. `retire()`'s only job is the per-session local cleanup.
- **Not addressed here, deliberately:** the "sweep-in-progress tracking" Decisions bullet above (each session "reports in" against a shared counter/set as it finishes) has no code yet - there is no snapshot, no counter, and nothing calls `retire()` from anywhere. That, plus the ignore-if-a-sweep-is-already-in-progress check itself, remain the next step.
- **Where:** `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/utils_session/session_worker.py` only.

#### Part 3, step 3 — sweep-in-progress tracking + accept/reject decision — implemented 2026-09-12, by explicit instruction ("implement session_reset inprogress status log for part 3 to reject in-coming session_reset_request if session_reset is still on-going")

Completes this entry's originally-designed accept/ignore behaviour - every prior step (1 and 2) was reachable but always accepted unconditionally, since nothing yet tracked whether a sweep was already running. This step wires the last piece: rejecting a second `session_clear_request` while an earlier one is still in progress, and actually calling `retire()` on every active session when one is accepted.

- [x] **Open Question 2 resolved: a `set[str]` (`_pending_retirements`), not a plain `int` counter.** Populated with every snapshotted `session_id` at accept-time, each entry `discard()`'d as that session reports its own retirement back in (`_report_retirement()`, called from `SessionWorker._run()`'s `retire()` exit branch). Its own non-emptiness *is* the "sweep in progress" signal - no separate boolean flag exists alongside it, avoiding two pieces of state that could ever disagree with each other.
- [x] **New `session_worker.py::handle_session_clear_request(task_id)`** - the accept/reject decision itself, called from `message_handler.py::_handle_session_clear_request()` once `task_id` has already been validated non-empty. Under `_sweep_lock`: if `_pending_retirements` is non-empty, rejects; otherwise snapshots every `SessionWorker` currently in `_sessions` (`_sessions_lock`, taken while still holding `_sweep_lock`) and adds all their `session_id`s to `_pending_retirements` in that same critical section, so a concurrent report-in can never interleave with a fresh snapshot. The actual publish (`session_clear_response()`) and the `retire()` signalling both happen *after* releasing `_sweep_lock`, deliberately - neither one needs to block whatever else might be reading/writing that set.
- [x] **New `session_worker.py::_reject_session_clear_request(task_id)`** - the ignore-path response. Publishes `{"task_id": task_id, "type": "error", "error_type": "session_reset_in_progress", "message": "A global session reset is already in progress - please try again once it finishes."}`.
  - **Decision made during implementation, not specified beforehand: `error`, not `completed`.** `completed` sends no reply at all on `telegram_gateway`'s side - the requesting admin would see nothing happen, with no way to tell a reset was already running versus their command simply being ignored/lost. `error` surfaces a clear, human-readable reason via `telegram_gateway`'s existing generic-`error_type` handling (any `error_type` other than `"token_exhausted"` sends `message` to the chat as-is - see `telegram_gateway/README.md`/`message_handler.py::_handle_error()`) - no `telegram_gateway`-side changes needed, since it's already fully generic. Open to reconsidering if `completed`'s silence is actually preferred.
- [x] **New `session_worker.py::_report_retirement(session_id)`** - called from `SessionWorker._run()`'s `retire()` exit branch (alongside `remove_session_worker()`/`clear_session_directory()`, already implemented in step 2), `discard()`s `session_id` from `_pending_retirements` under `_sweep_lock` and logs how many, if any, are still pending.
- [x] **Open Question 3 addressed: yes, the plain in-progress-flag-via-non-empty-set already covers the narrow accept/report-in race correctly by construction**, without any extra handling needed. The entire snapshot-and-populate step runs inside one `_sweep_lock` critical section, so a `session_clear_request` being decided and a `SessionWorker` reporting its own retirement can never observe or mutate `_pending_retirements` at the same time as each other - one fully happens, then the other. This application's own RabbitMQ consumer is also single-threaded (`utils_queue/queue.py::queue_consume_task()`), so two inbound `session_clear_request`s can never be *decided* concurrently with each other in the first place - `_sweep_lock`'s real job is only ever serialising one such decision against the independent background `SessionWorker` threads reporting in, never against another decision.
- [x] **Empty-registry case handled without special-casing:** if `_sessions` is empty at accept-time, `_pending_retirements` simply stays empty after the (no-op) update - `session_clear_response()` still publishes, nothing is signalled to `retire()`, and the very next `session_clear_request` is immediately accept-eligible again, since there was never anything to wait for.
- **Where:** `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/utils_session/session_worker.py` (all new state/functions), `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/utils_queue/message_handler.py` (`_handle_session_clear_request()` now calls `handle_session_clear_request()` instead of `session_clear_response()` directly).
- **Not addressed here at the time:** `README.md` documentation for the whole feature (deferred since step 1); the still-recorded "known interim limitation" from step 1 (a failed `session_clear_response()` publish leaves the admin's triggering `task_id` unclosed with no backstop, now also recorded in `README.md`'s own Limitations); and Open Question 4's §5 dependency, which remains genuinely blocked until the agent Call pipeline exists. **Update (2026-09-12): `README.md` documentation now added** - see the "Where" section below.

#### Part 3 — post-implementation review (2026-09-12): additional gaps identified, none fixed yet

A dedicated re-read of every step above, requested directly by the user ("relook into part 3... and determine what is yet to be covered") - a fresh critical pass over the actual code, not a re-statement of each step's own "[x]" markers. Also includes a full lock-inventory deadlock validation, requested in the same pass (see its own bullet below). None of the items here are fixed - recorded for follow-up, consistent with this file's own "flag, don't silently fix" convention elsewhere.

- **Reject-path publish failure has the identical unbackstopped gap already recorded for the accept path.** Step 1's "known interim limitation" bullet only calls out `session_clear_response()`'s own publish failure - `_reject_session_clear_request()` has the exact same shape (its own `publisher.publish()` can fail, logged via `logger.error()`, with nothing else closing the incoming `task_id` if it does). Not previously written down as its own item.
- **A continuously-busy session could delay its own retirement indefinitely, blocking every later `session_clear_request`.** `SessionWorker._run()` only checks `self._clear_event.is_set()` in the `except queue.Empty:` branch - i.e. only once `self.inbox.get(timeout=1)` actually times out with nothing queued. If new messages for that `session_id` keep arriving faster than that 1-second gap, the loop never reaches the check, the session never reports in via `_report_retirement()`, and `_pending_retirements` never empties - permanently rejecting every subsequent `session_clear_request`, not just delaying this one. Bounded in practice (needs a genuinely gapless, continuous stream from one chat - unlikely from ordinary human typing pace, and `_process_batch()` itself resolves near-instantly today since §5 doesn't exist yet), but a real, previously-unexamined edge case, structurally identical in shape to the accepted-but-narrow risks already recorded elsewhere in this file (e.g. §3's own "Stray message after `session_cleared`" open decision, below).
- **The existing "Stray message after `session_cleared`" accepted risk (§3's "Open decisions") now also applies to `retire()`, not only `session_cleared`.** That decision was written and accepted ("on hold, no change") before `retire()` existed. It's the same underlying mechanism - `get_or_create_session_worker()`'s create-if-missing behaviour resurrecting a worker for a message tagged with an already-removed `session_id` - just with a second trigger path now (a completed global reset via `retire()`, not only a single-session `session_cleared` ack). Not re-examined against this new path specifically; the existing "on hold" answer likely still applies, but this file should say so explicitly rather than leaving it implicit.
- **Full lock-inventory deadlock validation performed, no risk found.** Every `threading.Lock`/`RLock` in this application was enumerated (`queue.py::_lock_consume`, `session_worker.py::_sessions_lock`/`_sweep_lock`) and every acquisition site traced. The only lock-nesting relationship anywhere is `_sweep_lock` (outer) → `_sessions_lock` (inner), in exactly one place (`handle_session_clear_request()`), never reversed anywhere else - `_sessions_lock`'s three other acquisition sites (`get_or_create_session_worker()`, `remove_session_worker()`, `shutdown_all_session_workers()`) never themselves try to acquire `_sweep_lock`, so a circular wait is structurally impossible. Confirmed no blocking I/O is ever performed while either lock is held, and neither `with` block re-enters its own (non-reentrant) lock. `_lock_consume` is entirely isolated to `queue.py` and never interacts with either session lock. No code change made - this is a validation result, not a fix.
- **`telegram_gateway/CODE_TODO.md`'s own Part 3 entry was stale relative to this file** (still framed as "documented only" / not yet implemented) - reconciled the same day this review was performed; see that file's own Part 3 section.

#### `bot_started` — implemented 2026-09-12 (`telegram_gateway`'s Part 5, `bot_sanctuary` side)

Split out of this entry's original bundled scope and implemented independently of the rest (`session_clear_request`/`session_reset`/the dedicated clearing method remain open, per this entry's own §Blocking dependency and known-staleness notes above) — `bot_started` has no dependency on any of that, it is a plain, unconditional, no-payload restart broadcast.

- [x] **New `_push_bot_started()` (`utilities/initialise.py`)** — publishes `{"type": "bot_started"}` (no other fields) via its own disposable `RabbitMQPublisher`, same pattern as `resync_orphaned_sessions()`/`_close_intermediate_task_ids()` (`utils_session/session_worker.py`). Best-effort/non-fatal — a failed publish is logged and startup proceeds regardless, relying on `telegram_gateway`'s own `PENDING_RESET_MAX_WAIT_SECONDS` ceiling sweep as the fallback for a lost `bot_started`, exactly as that project's own `CODE_TODO.md` already expects.
- [x] **Open Question 5 resolved: fired after `initialise_rabbitmq_connection()`, still ahead of `resync_orphaned_sessions()`.** Not at the literal first line of `initialise_application()` as this entry's Decisions bullet above originally phrased it — `RabbitMQPublisher.publish()` only retries a bounded number of times (`Q_PUSH_MAX_ATTEMPTS`), unlike `initialise_rabbitmq_connection()`'s own indefinite startup retry, so firing this only once connectivity is already confirmed avoids a spurious failure against a broker that simply isn't up yet on a cold start. The ordering `telegram_gateway/CODE_TODO.md`'s Part 5 assumes (`bot_started` before `resync_orphaned_sessions()`/its own sweep) is preserved either way.
- [x] `initialise_application()` (`utilities/initialise.py`) updated: `test_llm_tokens()` → `initialise_rabbitmq_connection()` → `_push_bot_started()` → `resync_orphaned_sessions()` → `start_queue_consumer()`.
- No `README.md`/`message_handler.py` changes needed for this piece — `bot_started` is outbound-only from this application's side; nothing here consumes it.

#### Timed session reset (`SESSION_RESET_TIME`) — implemented 2026-09-12, `bot_sanctuary`-side only, per explicit correction

**Context - a first attempt at this was built in the wrong place and fully reverted.** The user asked for "a timed session reset retrieved from env variable" without naming a service. It was initially implemented entirely on `telegram_gateway`'s side instead - a new `SESSION_RESET_TIME` setting there, driving a new `trigger_timed_session_reset()` that pushed a `session_clear_request` (using the existing `SYSTEM_TRIGGERED_TASK_ID` sentinel as its `task_id`) exactly as if an external admin command had triggered it. Re-reading `telegram_gateway/CODE_TODO.md` immediately afterwards surfaced that this directly contradicted an already-agreed design recorded there on 2026-09-11/12 (its BUG entry's "Confirmed requirement" #2 and "Confirmed design correction" scenario 2, and Open Question 4): the scheduled/timed reset was always meant to be a **`bot_sanctuary`-side** mechanism, self-publishing `session_reset` directly (`task_id: null`) rather than going through the `session_clear_request` round-trip at all - `telegram_gateway` was never meant to have the authority to initiate a reset on a schedule, only to react to whichever of the two `session_reset` scenarios produced it. Flagged to the user rather than silently proceeding; user confirmed: **"revert all changes... implementation is meant for bot_sanctuary. telegram_gateway have no rights to trigger a session reset according to documentation."** Every `telegram_gateway`-side change from the first attempt (`config.py`, `session_reset_handler.py`, `initialise.py`, `README.md`) was reverted in full before this subsection's implementation began.

- [x] **New `config.py` setting: `SESSION_RESET_TIME`, plus a new `get_env_time()` helper**, same file/pattern as `get_env_int()`/`get_env_bool()` - silent fallback to `None` on unset/blank/unparsable (no logger exists in this file; `settings` is constructed before logging is configured). Accepts either a 24-hour value ("13:00", no am/pm suffix) or a 12-hour one ("1:00pm", with one). One deliberate quirk, per explicit instruction: an hour of 12 with **either** am/pm suffix - "12:00am" as much as "12:00pm" - is read as noon, never midnight; midnight can only be expressed via the 24-hour form ("00:00"). ~~Timezone-naive, by explicit instruction ("ignore timezone for now") - compared directly against the container's own local time.~~ **Superseded the same day - see the "Timezone awareness" subsection below.** The "ignore timezone for now" instruction was exactly that - deferring the question, not a permanent design choice - and the very next request asked for it directly.
- [x] **New `session_worker.py::_begin_session_clear_sweep()`** - the accept/reject decision and snapshot-then-mark-pending step from `handle_session_clear_request()` (Part 3, step 3) extracted into its own function, returning the snapshot (`list[SessionWorker]`, possibly empty) on accept or `None` on reject. `handle_session_clear_request(task_id)` itself is otherwise unchanged in behaviour, now just calling this helper rather than inlining the same logic. Done so the new timed trigger below shares the identical decision/locking rather than duplicating it.
- [x] **New `session_worker.py::trigger_timed_session_reset()`** - the scheduled trigger's own entry point, calling `_begin_session_clear_sweep()` exactly like `handle_session_clear_request()` does. On accept: publishes `session_reset` directly (new `_push_scheduled_session_reset()`, `{"task_id": None, "type": "session_reset"}`, own disposable `RabbitMQPublisher`, same pattern as `session_clear_response()`) then signals every snapshotted worker's `retire()`, identically to the accepted-`session_clear_request` path. On reject (a sweep is already in progress - another timed occurrence still running, or an admin-triggered `session_clear_request` still draining): silently skipped and logged, **not** rejected via a published `error` - there is no requesting `task_id` to notify of anything, unlike `_reject_session_clear_request()`'s reason for existing. The next scheduled occurrence (24h later) is the retry.
- [x] **New scheduler: `_seconds_until_next_session_reset_time()`, `_session_reset_schedule_loop()`, `start_session_reset_schedule()`, `stop_session_reset_schedule()`** (`session_worker.py`) - same shape as `telegram_gateway`'s own `start_pending_reset_ceiling_sweep()`/`stop_pending_reset_ceiling_sweep()` precedent (a `threading.Event`-gated background thread, recomputing its own wait duration every iteration rather than assuming a fixed 24h period, so it stays correct regardless of how far through the day it happened to start). `start_session_reset_schedule()` is a no-op (logged) if `SESSION_RESET_TIME` is unset - the default, meaning no timed reset at all.
- [x] **Wired into `initialise.py`**: `start_session_reset_schedule()` called after `resync_orphaned_sessions()`, ahead of `start_queue_consumer()`; `stop_session_reset_schedule()` called first in `terminate_application()`, ahead of `stop_queue_consumer()`.
- [x] **`README.md` updated** for this piece specifically (Startup/Shutdown narrative, mermaid diagram, new "Session Reset" settings table section) - not the same as the still-open, broader `session_clear_request`/`session_reset`/`bot_started` documentation gap noted in the "Part 3, step 3" subsection above, which remains open.
- **Not addressed here, deliberately out of scope for this turn:** the three gaps already recorded in the "Part 3 — post-implementation review" subsection above (unbackstopped reject-path publish failure, the continuously-busy-session edge case, the extended "stray message" risk) all apply equally to this new trigger, since it shares `_begin_session_clear_sweep()`/`retire()` with the existing path - no new equivalent gap was introduced, but none of the existing ones were fixed either.
- **Where:** `bot_sanctuary_application/config.py` (new setting + `get_env_time()`), `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/utils_session/session_worker.py` (new functions + refactor), `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/initialise.py` (wiring), `README.md` (Startup/Shutdown/settings table/diagram).

#### Timezone awareness (`TZ`) — implemented 2026-09-12, both `bot_sanctuary` and `telegram_gateway`

Direct follow-up instruction the same day as the "Timed session reset" subsection above: "implement timing according to whichever timezone set to env. follow `${HOME}/repository/V-Project-Multimedia-Application/development/compose.standalone_dev.yml` to implement timezone for both telegram_gateway and bot_sanctuary. Logging such use timezone timing too." Retires `SESSION_RESET_TIME`'s "ignore timezone for now" scope from the moment it was implemented earlier the same day - see that bullet's own superseded note above.

- [x] **New `config.py` setting: `TZ`, plus a new `get_env_timezone()` helper** (same file, same silent-fallback convention as `get_env_int()`/`get_env_bool()`/`get_env_time()`) - resolves the standard `TZ` environment variable (not a project-prefixed name) to a `zoneinfo.ZoneInfo`, falling back to `"UTC"` on unset or an unrecognised zone name. Deliberately reads the plain `TZ` name rather than e.g. `CHATBOT_TZ` directly, since that's also what the container's own OS layer already expects - `compose.dev.yml` sets the container's `TZ` env var and this application's `settings.TZ` from the exact same `CHATBOT_TZ` config.ini value, so the two can never disagree in practice.
- [x] **`session_worker.py::_seconds_until_next_session_reset_time()` made timezone-aware** - `datetime.now()` (naive) replaced with ~~`datetime.now(settings.TZ)` (aware)~~. **Superseded later the same day - see the "Centralised time retrieval" subsection below: `now` is now obtained from the new `application_time()` helper instead (still aware, still anchored to `settings.TZ`, just no longer called directly here).** `SESSION_RESET_TIME` itself remains a plain hour/minute with no timezone of its own (`get_env_time()`, unchanged) - it is `now.replace(hour=..., minute=...)`'d against the aware `now`, so it inherits `settings.TZ` at combination time rather than needing its own tzinfo. The "started timed session reset schedule" startup log line updated to print `settings.TZ` instead of the now-stale "(timezone ignored)".
- [x] **`logging_setup.py` (both `bot_sanctuary` and `telegram_gateway`) - `Formatter.converter` overridden** to `lambda timestamp: datetime.fromtimestamp(timestamp, tz=settings.TZ).timetuple()`, replacing the default `time.localtime` converter. Applied once, to the one `formatter` instance shared by both the console and rotating-file handlers. This makes every log timestamp in both applications explicit about which zone it's in, rather than relying on the container's OS layer correctly picking up its own `TZ` env var (which it does today, but this removes the dependency on that continuing to hold).
- [x] **`tzdata` (PyPI) added to both `requirements.txt` files.** `python:3.12.4-slim` (the base image for both applications, per `Dockerfile.dev`) has no guaranteed system IANA timezone database (`/usr/share/zoneinfo`) - Debian's `-slim` variants commonly omit `tzdata` to save size. `zoneinfo.ZoneInfo()` automatically falls back to the `tzdata` PyPI package when the system database is missing (documented Python stdlib behaviour, no code-side branching needed) - installing it removes the risk of `ZoneInfoNotFoundError` on a from-scratch slim image, independent of whatever the OS-level `TZ` env var situation happens to be.
- [x] **`config_sample.ini` - new `CHATBOT_TZ="Asia/Singapore"` variable**, placed in the shared/common section (alongside `CHATBOT_NAME`/`CHATBOT_NETWORK_NAME`) since it applies to both services, not scoped to one. Not added to `setup.sh`'s `get_masked_config_variables()` - that list is for values every deployment must supply its own real value for (secrets, tokens, chat IDs, the persona name); `TZ` already has one universally-reasonable default value carried over unchanged from what `compose.dev.yml` previously hardcoded, so it doesn't need per-install prompting the way those do. Not added to `telegram_gateway/setup.sh`'s `tg_dev_run_docker()`/`bot_sanctuary/setup.sh`'s `bs_dev_run_docker()` either, consistent with `SESSION_RESET_TIME`'s own precedent (see "Timed session reset" subsection above) - both settings have a safe, working default inside `config.py` itself, so a standalone (non-compose) dev run works correctly without needing every setting individually wired through as a `-e` flag; only settings with no safe default (credentials, tokens) get that treatment.
- [x] **`compose.dev.yml` - both services' hardcoded `TZ: "Asia/Singapore"` replaced with `TZ: "${CHATBOT_TZ}"`**, following the referenced `compose.standalone_dev.yml`'s own pattern of setting a plain `TZ` container env var, but parameterised through this project's existing `CHATBOT_*`/`config.ini` convention rather than a second hardcoded literal, so both services always agree with each other and with `config_sample.ini`'s own default.
- [x] **Both `README.md`s updated** - new `TZ`/`CHATBOT_TZ` settings-table entries (a new "Timezone" section in `telegram_gateway/README.md`; folded into the existing "Application / Logging" section in this file, since it sits right next to `LOG_LEVEL` et al.), plus the `SESSION_RESET_TIME` table entry's own stale "Timezone-naive" wording corrected in place.
- **Not addressed here, deliberately out of scope:** DST transition edge cases for `_seconds_until_next_session_reset_time()`'s own arithmetic (aware-datetime subtraction across a DST boundary using the same `zoneinfo` object is handled correctly by Python's own `datetime`/`zoneinfo` machinery for the common case, but this was not specifically stress-tested against a real DST-observing zone) - not raised as a concern, not fixed defensively either, since it wasn't asked for and the deployment target (`Asia/Singapore`) does not observe DST at all.
- **Where:** `bot_sanctuary_application/config.py` (new setting + `get_env_timezone()`), `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/utils_session/session_worker.py` (`_seconds_until_next_session_reset_time()`, startup log line), `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/logging_setup.py`, `bot_sanctuary/requirements.txt`, `bot_sanctuary/README.md`. Also, in `telegram_gateway`: `telegram_gateway_application/config.py` (new setting + `get_env_timezone()`), `telegram_gateway_application/utilities/logging_setup.py`, `telegram_gateway/requirements.txt`, `telegram_gateway/README.md`. Shared: root `config_sample.ini` (new `CHATBOT_TZ`), root `compose.dev.yml` (both services' `TZ` env var).

#### Centralised time retrieval — `application_time()`/`application_time_diff()` (`utilities/utilities.py`) — implemented 2026-09-12, both `bot_sanctuary` and `telegram_gateway`

Direct same-day follow-up instruction: "update config.ini too. Additionally, create application_time() function in utilitise.py on both bot_sanctuary and telegram_gateway. all retrieval of time should be retrieved from application_time(), create another function to get time diff too if required." Two parts: (1) `config.ini` itself (the real, generated-from-sample file, not just `config_sample.ini`) had been missed when `CHATBOT_TZ` was added earlier the same day - now caught up; (2) every direct `datetime.now()`/`time.time()` call in either application's own code is now routed through one shared helper instead, so every timing decision and every stored timestamp is guaranteed to agree on the same clock.

- [x] **`config.ini` updated** - the same `CHATBOT_TZ="Asia/Singapore"` block just added to `config_sample.ini` in the "Timezone awareness" subsection above, added to the real `config.ini` too (this file was overlooked in that earlier pass - `config_sample.ini` is the template `setup.sh` copies from, but `config.ini` is what `run_dev()` actually reads into `.env`).
- [x] **New `application_time() -> datetime` in `utilities/utilities.py` (both projects)** - returns `datetime.now(settings.TZ)`. Declared the single source of truth for "what time is it right now" - every module needing the current time calls this instead of `datetime.now()`/`time.time()` directly. Placed in `utilities.py` (not `config.py`) since it's a behaviour, not a setting, and `utilities.py` is this codebase's existing home for dependency-free shared helpers (`ShutdownSignal` already lives there).
- [x] **New `application_time_diff(reference: float) -> float` in the same file (both projects)** - returns `application_time().timestamp() - reference`, for callers that persist a bare epoch float (Redis/JSON-stored timestamps) and only need the elapsed duration back, not a fresh wall-clock value. Added because it was needed immediately - both projects already had exactly this "epoch float now, minus an earlier stored epoch float" pattern in production code (see below), not speculatively added ahead of need.
- [x] **Every existing direct time-retrieval call site migrated to the two functions above:**
  - `bot_sanctuary`: `utils_session/session_worker.py::_seconds_until_next_session_reset_time()`'s `now = datetime.now(settings.TZ)` → `now = application_time()`. `utils_redis/database.py::should_notify_gateway_alert()`/`mark_gateway_alert_notified()`'s `time.time()` calls → `application_time_diff(...)`/`application_time().timestamp()` respectively.
  - `telegram_gateway`: `utils_redis/database.py::set_pending_reset()`'s `"created_at": time.time()` → `"created_at": application_time().timestamp()`. `utils_session/session_reset_handler.py::_is_pending_reset_expired()`'s `time.time() - created_at` → `application_time_diff(created_at)`.
  - `time.sleep(...)` calls (retry backoff delays, both projects) deliberately left untouched - they are not a "retrieval of time", and `application_time()` has no sleep-equivalent to offer them.
  - `logging_setup.py`'s `Formatter.converter` lambda (both projects) deliberately left untouched - its `timestamp` argument is supplied by the stdlib `logging` module itself (each `LogRecord`'s own creation time), not retrieved by this codebase's own code, so there is nothing here for `application_time()` to replace.
- **Where:** `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/utilities.py`, `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/utils_session/session_worker.py`, `bot_sanctuary_application/utilities/utils_redis/database.py`; `telegram_gateway_application/utilities/utilities.py`, `telegram_gateway_application/utilities/utils_redis/database.py`, `telegram_gateway_application/utilities/utils_session/session_reset_handler.py`; root `config.ini`.

#### FIX — CCR-025: `get_env_timezone()` only caught one of several exception types `zoneinfo.ZoneInfo()` can raise for a malformed `TZ` value — implemented, both `bot_sanctuary` and `telegram_gateway`

Fixes the Medium-severity finding identified in `telegram_gateway/CODE_NON_COMPLIANCE.md`'s twelfth follow-up compliance review (2026-09-12, scoped to the "Timezone awareness" work above), by explicit instruction ("update fix for both telegram_gateway and bot_santuary") following an on-request validation pass that confirmed the finding was still Open. `bot_sanctuary` has no `CODE_NON_COMPLIANCE.md` of its own - this subsection is this project's own record of the fix; see `telegram_gateway/CODE_TODO.md`'s parallel "FIX — CCR-025" entry for the full finding writeup.

- [x] **`config.py::get_env_timezone()`'s `except` clause broadened from `ZoneInfoNotFoundError` alone to `(ZoneInfoNotFoundError, ValueError, OSError)`** - `zoneinfo.ZoneInfo()` also raises `ValueError` for an absolute-path/uplevel key (e.g. `"../etc/passwd"`) and `IsADirectoryError` (an `OSError` subclass) for a key naming a tzdata directory rather than a leaf zone (e.g. `TZ=America`, a plausible operator typo) - neither was previously caught, so either crashed the whole application at import time (`settings = Settings()` runs unconditionally before `setup_logging()` or any other guarded startup step) instead of falling back to `default` as the function's own docstring already promised.
- [x] **No behavioural change for a valid `TZ` value** - only the failure path is widened.
- [x] **Docstring's Notes updated** to name all three caught exception types and cross-reference CCR-025 by number.
- [x] **Applied identically to `telegram_gateway_application/config.py`'s own `get_env_timezone()`** - the two functions are byte-for-byte identical copies (no shared package between the two projects), so the same defect existed in both and both are fixed together.
- **Not addressed here:** whether `telegram_gateway/CODE_NON_COMPLIANCE.md` should be updated to mark CCR-025 Resolved is a call for whoever owns that document next - it's treated as an immutable compliance record and was not modified as part of this fix.
- **Where:** `bot_sanctuary_application/config.py::get_env_timezone()`, `telegram_gateway_application/config.py::get_env_timezone()`.

See the "Part 3, step 3" subsection above.**

Genuinely still blocked, though: the graceful-vs-immediate distinction this question asks about remains unverifiable against real behaviour until §5 exists.**

This entry's entire code scope is now complete**, bar the still-open items noted in the "Part 3, step 3" subsection above (`README.md`, the unfixed failed-publish limitation, and §5 itself).
